Listing 0:
Matlab implementation for our
Jacobi-Givens-QR
algorithm.

Please take note of the following four important points in Listing
F.1
.

a)

When we remove Lines 7-10 and Lines 13-15 from Listing
F.1
, it essentially reverts to the classical
Givens-QR
algorithm.
Givens-QR
operates by selecting an appropriate Givens rotation matrix
\mathbf{V}=[\begin{smallmatrix}\cos(\theta)&\sin(\theta)\\
-\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)\end{smallmatrix}]
with a suitable rotation angle
\theta
to zero-out the matrix element
\mathbf{R}_{ij}
systematically from left to right (
j=1\rightarrow n
) and bottom to top (
i=n\rightarrow(j+1)
) within every pair of neighboring columns.

b)

Lines 7-10 and Lines 13-15 can be viewed as correction steps to ensure that the entries
\mathbf{R}_{j,j}=1
for all
j={n}
.

c)

Line 7-10 is executed for
(n-2)
times. In Line 7-10, when
Jacobi-Givens-QR
detects a negative entry
\mathbf{R}_{i-1,i-1}
with
i=j+1
, it applies a rotation matrix
\mathbf{V}\triangleq(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix})
to the two rows
\texttt{{B}}=[i-1,i]
to ensure that
3
3

3

Alternatively, one can use the reflection matrix
\mathbf{V}\triangleq(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix})
instead of the rotation matrix
\mathbf{V}\triangleq(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix})
to ensure that
\mathbf{R}_{i-1,i-1}=1
.

\mathbf{R}_{i-1,i-1}=1
.

d)

Line 13-15 is executed only once when
\det(\mathbf{X})=-1
. In such cases, we have
\mathbf{R}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}=(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix})
and
\det(\mathbf{R}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}})=-1
, where
\texttt{{B}}=[n-1,n]
is the two indices for the final rotation or reflection step. To ensure that the resulting
\mathbf{R}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}
is an identify matrix,
Jacobi-Givens-QR
employs a reflection matrix
\mathbf{V}=(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix})
, leading to
\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{R}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}=\mathbf{I}_{2}
.

Therefore, we establish the conclusion that any orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,n)
can be expressed as
\mathbf{D}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}...\mathcal{W}_{2}\mathcal{W}_{1}
, where
\mathcal{W}_{i}=\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}+\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{i}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}
, and
\mathcal{V}_{i}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)
is a suitable matrix associated with
\mathcal{B}_{i}
. Furthermore, if
\forall i,\,\mathcal{V}_{i}=\mathbf{I}_{2}
, we have
\forall i,\,\mathcal{W}_{i}=\mathbf{I}_{n}
, leading to
\mathbf{D}=\mathbf{I}_{n}
. This concludes the proof of the first part of this theorem.

Part (b)
. For any given
\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
and
\mathbf{X}^{0}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
, we let:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{D}}=\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{St}(n,n)}(\mathbf{X}[\mathbf{X}^{0}]^{\mathsf{T}}),

(33)

where
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{St}(n,n)}(\mathbf{Y})
denotes the nearest orthogonality matrix to the given matrix
\mathbf{Y}
.

Assume that the matrix
\mathbf{X}[\mathbf{X}^{0}]^{\mathsf{T}}
has the following singular value decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}^{0})^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}},~\mathbf{z}\in\{0,1\}^{n},~\mathbf{U}\in\mathrm{St}(n,n),~\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(n,n).

Therefore, we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}[\mathbf{X}^{0}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{V}.

(34)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\in\mathrm{St}(n,n).

(35)

Furthermore, we derive the following results:

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})^{\mathsf{T}}=\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}[\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})^{\mathsf{T}}-\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})\mathrm{Diag}(\mathbf{z})^{\mathsf{T}}]\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}[\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{0}\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{U}-\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}^{0})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{0}\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{U}]\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ }}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{0}\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}^{0})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{0}\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{0}-\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{D}}\cdot\mathbf{X}^{0}-\mathbf{\mathbf{X}}=\mathbf{0},

where step ① uses (
34
); step ② uses
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{T}}=\mathbf{I}_{n}
,
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\mathsf{T}}=\mathbf{I}_{n}
,
\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, and
[\mathbf{X}^{0}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{0}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
; step ③ uses (
35
). We conclude that, for any given
\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
and
\mathbf{X}^{0}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
, we can always find a matrix
\bar{\mathbf{D}}\in\mathrm{St}(n,n)
such that
\bar{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{X}^{0}=\mathbf{X}
.

Since the matrix
\bar{\mathbf{D}}\in\mathrm{St}(n,n)
can be represented as
\bar{\mathbf{D}}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}...\mathcal{W}_{2}\mathcal{W}_{1}
, where
\mathcal{W}_{i}=\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\mathcal{V}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}+\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{i}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}
for some suitable
\mathcal{V}_{i}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)
(as established in the first part of this theorem), we can conclude that any matrix
\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
can be expressed as
\mathbf{X}=\bar{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{X}^{0}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}...\mathcal{W}_{2}\mathcal{W}_{1}\mathbf{X}^{0}
.

∎

F.2
Proof for Theorem
3.6

Proof.

We use
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
,
\ddot{\mathbf{X}}
, and
\check{\mathbf{X}}
to denote the
global optimal point
, BS
k
-
point
, and
critical point
of Problem (
1
), respectively.

Setting the Riemannian subgradient of
\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\ddot{\mathbf{X}},\texttt{{B}})

w.r.t.

\mathbf{V}
to zero, we have
\mathbf{0}\in\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\ddot{\mathbf{X}},\texttt{{B}})=\ddot{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{V})\ominus\mathbf{V}[\ddot{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{V})]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{V}
, where
\ddot{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{V})=\alpha(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k})+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\mathrm{mat}(\mathbf{H}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{X}^{+}-\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))+\nabla f(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})+\partial h(\mathbf{X}^{+})]\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}
and
\mathbf{X}^{+}=\ddot{\mathbf{X}}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}
. Letting
\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{I}_{k}
, we have the following
necessary but not sufficient
condition for any BS
k
-
point
:

\displaystyle\forall\texttt{{B}}\in\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}},~\mathbf{0}=\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathbf{G}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}-\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}},~\text{with}~\mathbf{G}\in\nabla f(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})+\partial h(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}).

(36)

Part (a)
. We now show that
\{\rm{critical\,points}\,\check{\mathbf{X}}\}\supseteq\{\rm{BS}_{k}\text{-}\rm{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}
for all
k\geq 2
. We let
\mathbf{G}\in\nabla f(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})+\partial h(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})
. Using Lemma
A.1
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{n,n}=\mathbf{G}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}-\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{0}_{n,n}\cdot\ddot{\mathbf{X}})=(\mathbf{G}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}-\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}})\ddot{\mathbf{X}}

(37)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{n,r}=\mathbf{G}-\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}},

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}\cdot\mathbf{0}_{n,r}=\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathbf{G}-\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{r,r}=\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{n,n}=\ddot{\mathbf{X}}(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}})\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{n,n}=\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\underbrace{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{G}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}}_{\triangleq\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}}-\underbrace{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}}_{\triangleq\mathbf{G}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}},

where steps
①
and
②
use
\ddot{\mathbf{X}}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
; step
③
uses Equality (
37
) that
\mathbf{G}=\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{G}^{\mathsf{T}}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}
. We conclude that the necessary condition in Equation (
36
) is equivalent to the optimality condition of critical points.

Part (b)
. We now show that
\{\rm{BS}_{2}\text{-}\rm{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}\supseteq\{\rm{global\,optimal\,points}\,\bar{\mathbf{X}}\}
. We define
\mathcal{X}^{\star}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V})\triangleq\bar{\mathbf{X}}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{X}}
, and
\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X},\texttt{{B}})\triangleq f(\mathbf{X})+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X})(\mathbf{X})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}+h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X})+h(\mathbf{V}\mathbf{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X})
. We let
\mathbf{V}_{(i)}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)
and
\mathcal{B}_{i}\in\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}
. We derive:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{2};\bar{\mathbf{X}},\mathcal{B}_{i}),\,\forall\mathcal{B}_{i}

(38)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle F(\bar{\mathbf{X}})=h(\bar{\mathbf{X}})+f(\bar{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle h(\mathbf{X})+f(\mathbf{X}),\forall\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\textstyle h(\bar{\mathbf{X}}+\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}(\mathbf{V}_{(i)}-\mathbf{I})\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{X}})+f(\bar{\mathbf{X}}+\mathrm{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}(\mathbf{V}_{(i)}-\mathbf{I})\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{X}}),\,\forall\mathbf{V}_{(i)},~\forall\mathcal{B}_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle h(\mathcal{X}^{\star}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}(\mathbf{V}_{(i)}))+f(\mathcal{X}^{\star}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}(\mathbf{V}_{(i)})),\,\forall\mathbf{V}_{(i)},~\forall\mathcal{B}_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V}_{(i)};\bar{\mathbf{X}},\mathcal{B}_{i}),\,\forall\mathbf{V}_{(i)},~\forall\mathcal{B}_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\bar{\mathbf{X}},\mathcal{B}_{i}),\,\forall\mathcal{B}_{i},

where step
①
uses the definition of
\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X},\texttt{{B}})\triangleq f(\mathbf{X})+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X})(\mathbf{X})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}+h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X})+h(\mathbf{V}\mathbf{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X})
; step
②
uses the definition of
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
; step
③
uses the basis representation of orthogonal matrices when
k=2
, as shown in Theorem
3.1
; step
④
uses the definition of
\mathcal{X}^{\star}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V})
; step
⑤
uses the same strategy as in deriving Inequality (
2
). This leads to:

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{2}\in\arg\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)}~\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\bar{\mathbf{X}},\mathcal{B}_{i}),~\forall\mathcal{B}_{i}.

The inclusion above implies that
\{\rm{BS}_{2}\text{-}\rm{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}\supseteq\{\rm{global\,optimal\,points}\,\bar{\mathbf{X}}\}
.

Part (c)
. We now show that
\{\rm{BS}_{k}\text{-}\textit{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}\supseteq\{\rm{BS}_{k+1}\text{-}\textit{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}
. It is evident that the subproblem of finding
\rm{BS}_{k}\text{-}\textit{points}
is encompassed within that of finding
\rm{BS}_{k+1}\text{-}\textit{points}
stationary point. Thus, we conclude that the optimality of the latter is stronger.

Part (d)
. The inclusion
\{\rm{critical\,points}\,\check{\mathbf{X}}\}\subseteq\{\rm{BS}_{k}\text{-}\rm{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}
may not always hold true. This can be illustrated through simple examples of
2\times 2
optimization problems under orthogonality constraints (see Appendix Section
C.1
for more details). Lastly, it is also evident that the inclusions
\{\rm{BS}_{2}\text{-}\rm{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}\subseteq\{\rm{global\,optimal\,points}\,\bar{\mathbf{X}}\}
and
\{\rm{BS}_{k}\text{-}\textit{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}\subseteq\{\rm{BS}_{k+1}\text{-}\textit{points}\,\ddot{\mathbf{X}}\}
may not always hold true.

∎

Appendix G
Proof for Section
4

G.1
Proof for Theorem
4.2

Proof.

We define
\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}})\triangleq\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}+h(\mathbf{V}\mathbf{Z})+\langle\mathbf{V},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle+\ddot{c}
, where
\mathbf{Z}\triangleq\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}
and
\ddot{c}=h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})+f(\mathbf{X}^{t})-\langle\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle
is a constant.

We define
\tilde{c}\triangleq\textstyle\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot(F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))
.

Part (a)
. First, we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle h(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})-h(\mathbf{X}^{t})\overset{\text{①}}{=}~

\displaystyle h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})-h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}~

\displaystyle h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})+h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})-h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})-h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}~

\displaystyle h(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})-h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}),

(39)

where step ① uses
\mathbf{X}^{t+1}=\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\mathbf{V}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}
as in (
4
) and
\mathbf{I}=\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{c}}^{\mathsf{T}}
; step ② and step ③ use the coordinate-wise separable structure of
h(\cdot)
.

Second, since
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\in\arg\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}})
, it follows that
\mathcal{K}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}})\leq\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}})
. This further leads to:

\displaystyle h(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t})+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}+\langle\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle\leq h(\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}).

(40)

Third, we denote
\mathbf{X}^{t+1}=\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t})
and derive:

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})-f(\mathbf{X}^{t})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t})-\mathbf{X}^{t},\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t})-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}}

(41)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t},\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|^{2}_{\underline{\mathbf{Q}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{Q}},

where step
①
uses Inequality (
2
); step
②
uses Claim (
a
) of Lemma
2.2
; step
③
uses
\mathbf{Q}\succcurlyeq\underline{\mathbf{Q}}
.

Adding (
G.1
), (
40
), and (
41
) together, we obtain the following sufficient decrease condition:

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})-F(\mathbf{X}^{t})\leq-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{X}^{t+1}-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2},

(42)

where step ① uses Claim (
c
) of Lemma
2.2
.

Part (b)
. We assume that
\texttt{{B}}^{t}
is selected from
\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\}
{}_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}
randomly and uniformly.

Taking the expectation for Inequality (
42
), we obtain a lower bound on the expected progress made by each iteration:

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})]-F(\mathbf{X}^{t})\leq-\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}].

Telescoping the inequality above over
t=0,1,...,T
, we have:

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T}}[\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T}}[F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\mathbf{X}^{T+1})]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T}}[F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})],

where
\ddot{\mathbf{X}}
denotes the limit point of Algorithm
1
. As a result, there exists an index
\bar{t}
with
0\leq\bar{t}\leq T
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{\bar{t}}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]\leq\tfrac{2}{\alpha(T+1)}[F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})]=\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{T+1}.

(43)

Furthermore, for any
t
,
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}
is the optimal solution of the following minimization problem at
\mathbf{X}^{t}
:
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\in\arg\min_{\mathbf{V}}\min_{\mathbf{V}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}}^{t})
. Given
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}
is a random output matrix depends on the observed realization of the random variable
\texttt{{B}}^{t}
, we directly obtain the following equality:

\displaystyle\textstyle\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\text{dist}(\mathbf{I}_{k},\arg\min_{\mathbf{V}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X}^{t},\mathcal{B}_{i}))^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}].

(44)

Combining (
43
) and (
44
), we conclude that there exists an index
\bar{t}
with
\bar{t}\in[0,T]
such that the associated solution
\mathbf{X}^{\bar{t}}
qualifies as an
\epsilon
-BS
k
-
point
of Problem (
1
), provided that
T
is sufficiently large such that
\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{T+1}\leq\epsilon
. We conclude that
OBCD
finds an
\epsilon
-BS
k
-
point
of Problem (
1
) in at most
T
iterations deterministically, where
T\geq\lceil\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{\epsilon}-1\rceil
.

Part (c)
. We assume that
\texttt{{B}}^{t}
is selected from
\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\}
{}_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}
cyclically, i.e.,
\mathcal{B}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{3}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}-1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{3}\rightarrow\ldots
.

Telescoping Inequality (
42
) over
t
from
0
to
T
yields:

\displaystyle\textstyle\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T}||\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}||_{F}^{2}\leq F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\mathbf{X}^{T+1})\leq F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}),

(45)

For notation simplicity, we define
z\triangleq\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}
and
\dot{e}^{t}\triangleq\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}
. We have from Inequality (
45
):

\displaystyle\textstyle\tilde{c}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\textstyle(F(\mathbf{X}^{0})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))\cdot\tfrac{2}{\alpha}

(46)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}\dot{e}^{t}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\dot{e}^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{z}\dot{e}^{i}+\sum_{i=z+1}^{2z}\dot{e}^{i}+\sum_{i=2z+1}^{3z}\dot{e}^{i}+\ldots

\displaystyle\textstyle+\sum_{i=[\lfloor T/z\rfloor-1]z+1}^{\lfloor T/z\rfloor z}\dot{e}^{i}+\sum_{i=\lfloor T/z\rfloor z+1}^{T}\dot{e}^{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{z}\dot{e}^{i}+\sum_{i=z+1}^{2z}\dot{e}^{i}+\sum_{i=2z+1}^{3z}\dot{e}^{i}+\ldots+\sum_{i=[\lfloor T/z\rfloor-1]z+1}^{\lfloor T/z\rfloor z}\dot{e}^{i}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\textstyle(\min_{k=1}^{\lfloor T/z\rfloor}[{\sum_{i=(k-1)z+1}^{kz}}\dot{e}^{i}])\times\lfloor T/z\rfloor,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\geq}

\displaystyle\textstyle(\min_{k=1}^{\lfloor T/z\rfloor}[{\sum_{i=(k-1)z+1}^{kz}}\dot{e}^{i}])\times(\tfrac{T-z}{z}),

where step
①
uses
\dot{e}^{i}\geq 0
for all
i
, and
T\geq\lfloor T/z\rfloor z
for all
T\geq 0
; step
②
uses
\lfloor T/z\rfloor\geq\tfrac{T}{z}-1
for all
T>z>0
. Inequality (
46
) implies that there exists an index
\bar{k}
with
\bar{k}\in[1,\lfloor T/z\rfloor]
satisfying

\displaystyle\textstyle\tfrac{1}{z}{\sum_{i=1+(\bar{k}-1)z}^{\bar{k}z}}\dot{e}^{i}\leq\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{T-z}.

(47)

Such inequality further implies the associated solution
\mathbf{X}^{\bar{k}z}
qualifies as an
\epsilon
-BS
k
-
point
of Problem (
1
), provided that
T
is sufficiently large such that
\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{T-z}\leq\epsilon
and
T>z
. We conclude that
OBCD
finds an
\epsilon
-BS
k
-
point
of Problem (
1
) in at most
T
iterations deterministically, where
T\geq\lceil\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{\epsilon}+z\rceil
.

∎

G.2
Proof of Lemma
4.4

Proof.

For notation simplicity, we define:
\|\partial F(\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\inf_{\mathbf{Y}\in\partial F(\mathbf{X})}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{\mathsf{F}}={\rm{dist}}(\mathbf{0},\partial F(\mathbf{X}))
.

We define
\mathbb{A}\ominus\mathbb{B}
as the element-wise subtraction between sets
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
.

We let
\mathds{H}^{t+1}\in\partial h(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})
, and define:

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}][\mathbf{X}^{t+1}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},

(48)

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}][\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},

(49)

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})-\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})][\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}.

(50)

Part (a)
. First, using the optimality of
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}
for the subproblem, we have:

\displaystyle\textbf{0}_{k,k}=\tilde{\mathbf{G}}-\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\tilde{\mathbf{G}}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{G}}=\underbrace{\mathrm{mat}((\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}))}_{\triangleq\Upsilon_{1}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}](\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}}_{\triangleq\Upsilon_{2}}.

Using the relation that
\tilde{\mathbf{G}}=\Upsilon_{1}+\Upsilon_{2}
, we obtain the following results from the above equality:

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{k,k}=(\Upsilon_{1}+\Upsilon_{2})-\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}(\Upsilon_{1}+\Upsilon_{2})^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}

(51)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\Rightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{k,k}=\Upsilon_{1}+\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}-\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}(\Upsilon_{1}+\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2})^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}=\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}(\Upsilon_{1}+\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2})^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Upsilon_{1}-\Omega_{2},

where step ① uses
\Upsilon_{2}=\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}
.

Second, since both
\texttt{{B}}^{t}
and
\texttt{{B}}^{t+1}
are randomly and dependently selected from
\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\}
{}_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}
with replacement, each with an equal probability of
\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}
, for any
\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, we have:

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathbb{E}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t+1}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{{\texttt{{B}}}^{t+1}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\mathrm{U}_{{\texttt{{B}}}^{t+1}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\|\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{\tilde{A}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}.

(52)

Third, we derive the following results:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t+1}}[{\rm{dist}}(\mathbf{0},\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t+1},\texttt{{B}}^{t+1}))]=\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t+1}}[\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t+1},\texttt{{B}}^{t+1})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

(53)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t+1}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{{\texttt{{B}}}^{t+1}}^{\mathsf{T}}\{\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})[\mathbf{X}^{t+1}]^{\mathsf{T}}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t+1}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})]^{\mathsf{T}}\}\mathrm{U}_{{\texttt{{B}}}^{t+1}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}\{\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})[\mathbf{X}^{t+1}]^{\mathsf{T}}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t+1}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})]^{\mathsf{T}}\}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{0}-\Omega_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{0}-\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{1}-\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{=}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{0}-\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}(\Upsilon_{1}+\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2})^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Upsilon_{1}-\Omega_{2}-\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑥}}{=}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{0}-\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Upsilon_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Upsilon_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\textstyle+\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{2}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Omega_{2}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]~~~~

where step ① uses the definition of
\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X}^{t+1},\texttt{{B}}^{t+1})
at the point
\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{I}_{k}
; step ② uses Equality (
52
) with
\mathbf{\tilde{A}}=\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})^{\mathsf{T}}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t+1}(\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1}))^{\mathsf{T}}
; step ③ uses the definition of
\Omega_{0}
in Equation (
48
); step ④ uses Lemma
A.2
; step ⑤ uses Equality (
51
); step ⑥ uses the triangle inequality.

We now establish individual bounds for each term in Inequality (
53
). For the first term
2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{0}-\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]
in (
53
), we have:

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{0}-\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

(54)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}][\mathbf{X}^{t+1}-\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}][\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2(l_{f}+l_{h})\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}],

where step ① uses
\mathbf{X}^{t+1}=\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}
; step ② uses the inequality
\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{Y}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}
for all
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
repeatedly, and the fact that
\forall\mathbf{X},\|\nabla f(\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq l_{f},\|\partial h(\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq l_{h}
.

For the second term
\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Upsilon_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Upsilon_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]
in (
53
), we have::

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Upsilon_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Upsilon_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

(55)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Upsilon_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Upsilon_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Upsilon_{1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\mathrm{mat}((\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}))\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2(L_{f}+\alpha)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

where step ① uses the triangle inequality; step ② uses the inequality
\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{Y}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}
for all
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
; step ③ uses the definition of
\Omega_{1}
in (
49
); step ④ uses the fact that
\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq L_{f}
.

For the third term
\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]
in (
53
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

(56)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})+(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I})\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\cdot\|(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\cdot\|(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2(l_{f}+l_{h})\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|(\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

where step ① uses the fact that
-\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{I}_{k}+\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}=\mathbf{0}
; step ② uses the norm inequality; step ③ uses the fact that
\|\Omega_{1}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}=\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}][\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq\|\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})+\mathds{H}^{t+1}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}
which can be derived using the norm inequality; step ④ uses the fact that
\forall\mathbf{X},\|\nabla f(\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq l_{f},\|\partial h(\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq l_{h}
.

For the fourth term
\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{2}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Omega_{2}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]
in (
53
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{2}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\Omega_{2}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

(57)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\Omega_{2}^{\mathsf{T}}\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\mathbb{E}[\|\Omega_{2}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\Omega_{2}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})-\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})][\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{=}

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})-\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{=}

\displaystyle 2L_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\mathbf{X}^{t}-\mathbf{X}^{t+1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑥}}{=}

\displaystyle 2L_{f}\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}],

where step ① uses the triangle inequality; step ② uses the norm inequality; step ③ uses the definition of
\Omega_{2}=\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})-\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})][\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}
in (
50
); step ④ uses the norm inequality; step ⑤ uses the fact that
\nabla f(\mathbf{X})
is
L_{f}
-Lipschitz continuous; step ⑥ uses Claim (
c
) of Lemma
2.2
.

In view of (
54
), (
55
), (
56
), (
57
), and (
53
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t+1}}[\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t+1},\texttt{{B}}^{t+1})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]\leq\underbrace{(c_{1}+c_{2}+c_{3}+c_{4})}_{\triangleq\phi}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}],

where
c_{1}=2(l_{f}+l_{h})
,
c_{2}=2(L_{f}+\alpha)
,
c_{3}=2(l_{f}+l_{h})
, and
c_{4}=2L_{f}
.

Part (b)
. we show that
\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[{\rm{dist}}(\mathbf{0},\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{t}))]\leq\gamma\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[{\rm{dist}}(\mathbf{0},\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}}^{t}))]
, where
\gamma\triangleq({\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}/{\mathrm{C}_{n-2}^{k-2}})^{1/2}
. For all
\mathbf{D}^{t}\triangleq\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})]^{\mathsf{T}}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{D}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}(\mathbf{D}^{t}_{ij})^{2}+\sum_{i}\sum_{j=i}(\mathbf{D}^{t}_{ij})^{2}

(58)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}(\mathbf{D}^{t}_{ij})^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}_{n-2}^{k-2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\|\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{D}^{t}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}_{n-2}^{k-2}}\cdot\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}\mathbb{E}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{D}^{t}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{D}^{t}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}],

where step ① uses the fact that
\mathbf{D}^{t}_{ii}=0
for all
i\in[n]
; step ② uses Claim (
a
) of this lemma with
\mathbf{D}^{t}_{ii}=0
for all
i\in[n]
; step ③ uses
\mathbb{E}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{W}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]=\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}\|\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{U}_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}
as
\texttt{{B}}^{t}
are chosen from
\{\mathcal{B}_{i}\}
{}_{i=1}^{\mathrm{C}_{n}^{k}}
randomly and uniformly;
④
uses the definition of
\gamma
. We further derive:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{t})\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\|\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

(59)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle\|\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\|\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})]^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{=}

\displaystyle\gamma\mathbb{E}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}[\|\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}^{\mathsf{T}}\{\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\ominus\mathbf{X}^{t}[\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})]^{\mathsf{T}}\}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}^{t}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{=}

\displaystyle\gamma\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}}^{t})\|_{\mathsf{F}}

where step
①
uses the definition of
\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{t}))
; step
②
uses
[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
; step
③
uses the inequality that
\|\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}
for all
\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
; step
④
uses Equality (
58
); step
⑤
uses the definition of
\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}}^{t})
.

∎

G.3
Proof of Theorem
4.6

Proof.

We derive the following results:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T}}[{\rm{dist}}^{2}(\mathbf{0},\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{T+1}))]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T+1}}[{\rm{dist}}^{2}(\mathbf{0},\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{T+1},\texttt{{B}}^{T+1}))]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\cdot\phi^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{T}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{T}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\cdot\phi^{2}\cdot\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{T+1},

where step
①
uses Lemma
4.4
(
b
); step
②
uses Lemma
4.4
(
a
); step
③
uses Inequality (
43
).

Therefore, we conclude that there exists an index
\bar{t}
with
\bar{t}\in[0,T]
such that the associated solution
\mathbf{X}^{\bar{t}}
qualifies as an
\epsilon
-
critical point
of Problem (
1
) satisfying
\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{\bar{t}}}[{\rm{dist}}^{2}(\mathbf{0},\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{\bar{t}+1}))]\leq\epsilon
, provided that
T
is sufficiently large to ensure
\gamma^{2}\cdot\phi^{2}\cdot\tfrac{\tilde{c}}{T+1}\leq\epsilon
.

∎

G.4
Proof of Theorem
4.10

Proof.

Initially, given
F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X})\triangleq F(\mathbf{X})+\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{X})
is a KL function by our assumption, we can conclude, from Proposition
4.9
, that:

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{\prime}(F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F^{\circ}(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))}\leq\text{dist}(0,\partial F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X}^{t})).

(60)

Since
\varphi(\cdot)
is a concave desingularization function, we have:
\varphi(b)+(a-b)\varphi^{\prime}(a)\leq\varphi(a)
. Applying the inequality above with
a=F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})
and
b=F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})
, we have:

\displaystyle(F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1}))\varphi^{\prime}(F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))\leq\underbrace{\varphi(F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))-\varphi(F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))}_{\triangleq\mathcal{E}^{t}}.

(61)

Part (a)
. We define
\varphi^{t}\triangleq\varphi(F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))
. We derive the following inequalities:

\displaystyle(e^{t+1})^{2}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\tfrac{\mathcal{E}^{t}}{\varphi^{\prime}(F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\mathcal{E}^{t}\cdot\text{dist}(0,\partial F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X}^{t}))]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\mathcal{E}^{t}\cdot\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{t})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\mathcal{E}^{t}\gamma\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}}^{t})\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑥}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t-1}}[\mathcal{E}^{t}\gamma\phi\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t-1}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑦}}{=}

\displaystyle~\underbrace{\tfrac{2}{\alpha}\cdot\gamma\phi}_{\triangleq\kappa}\cdot(\varphi^{t}-\varphi^{t+1})\cdot e^{t},

where step ① uses the sufficient decrease condition as shown in Theorem
4.2
; step ② uses Inequality (
61
); step ③ uses Inequality (
60
); step ④ uses Lemma
A.7
; step ⑤ uses Inequality (
59
); step ⑥ uses Lemma
4.4
; step ⑦ uses the definitions of
\{\kappa,\varphi^{t},e^{t},\mathcal{E}^{t}\}
.

Part (b)
. Applying Lemma
A.10
with
p^{t}=\kappa\varphi^{t}
with
p^{t}\geq p^{t+1}
, for all
i\geq 1
, we have:

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{j=i}^{\infty}e^{j+1}\leq e^{i}+2p^{i}.

Using the definition of
d^{t}\triangleq\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}e^{j+1}
and letting
i=t
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\textstyle d^{t}\leq e^{t}+2p^{t}\overset{\text{①}}{=}e^{t}+2\kappa\varphi^{t}\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}e^{t}+2\kappa\varphi^{1}\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}2\sqrt{k}+2\kappa\varphi^{1},

where step ① uses
p^{t}=\kappa\varphi^{t}
; step ② uses
\varphi^{t}\leq\varphi^{1}
; step ③ uses
e^{t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t-1}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t-1}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t-1}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t-1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]+\|\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\leq\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{k}
. We conclude that
d^{t}\triangleq\sum_{j=t}^{\infty}e^{j+1}
is always upper-bounded.

Using the fact that
\|\mathbf{X}^{t+1}-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}\leq\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}
as shown in Lemma
2.2
(
c
), we conclude that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{i}}[\|\mathbf{X}^{i+1}-\mathbf{X}^{i}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]
is also always upper-bounded.

∎

G.5
Proof of Theorem
4.11

Proof.

We define
\varphi^{t}\triangleq\varphi(s^{t})
, where
s^{t}\triangleq F(\mathbf{X}^{t})-F(\ddot{\mathbf{X}})
.

We define
e^{t+1}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}]
, and
d^{i}=\sum_{j=i}^{\infty}e^{j+1}
.

First, we have:

\displaystyle\textstyle\|\mathbf{X}^{T}-\mathbf{X}^{\infty}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{j=T}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{X}^{j}-\mathbf{X}^{j+1}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{j=T}^{\infty}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{j}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{j=T}^{\infty}e^{j+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle d^{T},

where step
①
uses the triangle inequality; step
②
uses
\|\mathbf{X}^{t+1}-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}\leq\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}
, as shown in Lemma
2.2
(
c
); step
③
uses the definition of
e^{t+1}
; step
④
uses the definition of
d^{T}
. Therefore, it suffices to establish the convergence rate of
d^{T}
.

Second, we obtain the following results:

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{\prime}(s^{t})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\|{\rm{dist}}(\mathbf{0},\partial F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X}^{t}))\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{X}^{t})\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\gamma\|\partial_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{I}_{k};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}}^{t})\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t}}[\gamma\phi\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t-1}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\gamma\phi e^{t},

where step
①
uses uses Proposition
4.9
that
{\rm{dist}}(\mathbf{0},\partial F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}))\varphi^{\prime}(F^{\circ}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime})-F^{\circ}(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}))\geq 1
; step
②
uses Lemma
A.7
; step
③
uses Inequality (
59
); step
④
uses the Riemannian subgradient lower bound for the iterates gap in Lemma
4.4
; step
⑤
uses the definition of
e^{t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{t-1}}[\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t-1}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]
.

Third, using the definition of
d^{t}
, we derive:

\displaystyle\textstyle d^{t}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\triangleq}

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{i=t}^{\infty}e^{i+1}

(62)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\textstyle e^{t}+2\kappa\varphi^{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle e^{t}+2\kappa c\cdot\{[s^{t}]^{\sigma}\}^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle e^{t}+2\kappa c\cdot\{c(1-\sigma)\cdot\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{\prime}(s^{t})}\}^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle e^{t}+2\kappa c\cdot\{c(1-\sigma)\cdot\gamma\phi e^{t}\}^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{⑤}}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle d^{t-1}-d^{t}+2\kappa c\cdot\{c(1-\sigma)\cdot\gamma\phi(d^{t-1}-d^{t})\}^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}

\displaystyle\textstyle d^{t-1}-d^{t}+\underbrace{2\kappa c\cdot[c(1-\sigma)\gamma\phi]^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}}_{\triangleq\ddot{\kappa}}\cdot\{d^{t-1}-d^{t}\}^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}},

where step
①
uses
\sum_{i=t}^{\infty}e^{i+1}\leq e^{t}+2\kappa\varphi^{t}
, as shown in Theorem
4.10
(
b
); step
②
uses the definitions that
\varphi^{t}\triangleq\varphi(s^{t})
, and
\varphi(s)=cs^{1-\sigma}
; step
③
uses
\varphi^{\prime}(s)=c(1-\sigma)\cdot[s]^{-\sigma}
, leading to
[s^{t}]^{\sigma}=c(1-\sigma)\cdot\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{\prime}(s^{t})}
; step
④
uses Inequality (
G.5
); step
⑤
uses the fact that
e^{t}=d^{t-1}-d^{t}
.

We consider three cases for
\sigma\in[0,1)
.

Part (a)
. We consider
\sigma=0
. We have from Inequality (
G.5
):

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{\varphi^{\prime}(s^{t})}+\gamma\phi e^{t}

(63)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{c(1-\sigma)\cdot[s^{t}]^{-\sigma}}+\gamma\phi e^{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{c}+\gamma\phi e^{t},

where step
①
uses
\varphi^{\prime}(s)=c(1-\sigma)\cdot[s]^{-\sigma}
; step
②
uses
\sigma=0
and
e^{t}=d^{t-1}-d^{t}
.

Since
e^{t}\rightarrow 0
, and
\gamma,\phi,c>0
, Inequality (
63
) results in a contradiction
e^{t}\geq\tfrac{1}{c\gamma\phi}>0
. Therefore, there exists
t^{\prime}
such that
d^{t}=0
for all
t>t^{\prime}
, ensuring that the algorithm terminates in a finite number of steps.

Part (b)
. We consider
\sigma\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}]
. We let
t^{\prime}\triangleq\{i\,|\,d^{i-1}-d^{i}\leq 1\}
. For all
t\geq t^{\prime}
, we have from Inequality (
62
):

\displaystyle d^{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d^{t-1}-d^{t}+(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}\cdot\ddot{\kappa}

(64)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle d^{t-1}-d^{t}+(d^{t-1}-d^{t})\cdot\ddot{\kappa}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}

\displaystyle d^{t-1}\cdot\tfrac{\ddot{\kappa}+1}{\ddot{\kappa}+2},

where step
①
uses the fact that
[\Delta^{(1-\sigma)/\sigma}]/\Delta=\Delta^{(1-2\sigma)/\sigma}=\Delta^{(1/\sigma-2)}\leq\Delta^{0}=1
for all
\Delta=d^{t-1}-d^{t}\in[0,1]
and
\sigma\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2}]
. Therefore, we have:

\displaystyle d^{T}\leq d^{1}\cdot\left(\tfrac{\ddot{\kappa}+1}{\ddot{\kappa}+2}\right)^{T-1}.

Part (c)
. We consider
\sigma\in(\tfrac{1}{2},1)
. We define
w\triangleq\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}\in(0,1)
, and
\tau\triangleq 1/w-1\in(0,\infty)
.

We let
R
be any positive constant such that
e^{t}\leq R
for all
t\geq 1
.

For all
t\geq 2
, we have from Inequality (
62
):

\displaystyle d^{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d^{t-1}-d^{t}+\ddot{\kappa}\cdot(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle\ddot{\kappa}(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{w}+(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{w}\cdot(e^{t})^{1-w}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\ddot{\kappa}(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{w}+(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{w}\cdot R^{1-w}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}

\displaystyle(d^{t-1}-d^{t})^{w}\cdot\underbrace{(\ddot{\kappa}+R^{1-w})}_{\triangleq\dot{\kappa}},

where step
①
uses the definition of
w
and the fact that
d^{t-1}-d^{t}=e^{t}
; step
②
uses the fact that
\max_{x\in(0,R]}x^{1-w}\leq R^{1-w}
if
w\in(0,1)
and
R>0
. We further obtain:

\displaystyle\underbrace{[d^{t}]^{1/w}}_{=[d^{t}]^{\tau+1}}\leq(d^{t-1}-d^{t})\cdot{\dot{\kappa}}^{1/w}.

Applying Lemma
A.11
with
a={\dot{\kappa}}^{1/w}
, we have:

\displaystyle d^{T}\leq\mathcal{O}(T^{-1/\tau})\overset{\text{①}}{=}\mathcal{O}(T^{-\tfrac{1}{1/w-1}})\overset{\text{②}}{=}\mathcal{O}(T^{-\tfrac{1}{\tfrac{\sigma}{1-\sigma}-1}})=\mathcal{O}(T^{-\tfrac{1-\sigma}{2\sigma-1}}),

where step
①
uses
\tau\triangleq 1/w-1
; step
②
uses
w\triangleq\tfrac{1-\sigma}{\sigma}
.

∎

Appendix H
Proof for Section
5

H.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Proof.

We define
w\triangleq c-e
. We define
\breve{F}(\tilde{c},\tilde{s})\triangleq a\tilde{c}+b\tilde{s}+c\tilde{c}^{2}+d\tilde{c}\tilde{s}+e\tilde{s}^{2}+h(\tilde{c}\mathbf{x}+\tilde{s}\mathbf{y})
.

Initially, using
\sin^{2}(\theta)=1-\cos^{2}(\theta)
, we obtain the following problem, which is equivalent to Problem (
11
):

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}\in\arg\min_{\theta}a\cos(\theta)+b\sin(\theta)+w\cos^{2}(\theta)+d\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)+e+h(\cos(\theta)\mathbf{x}+\sin(\theta)\mathbf{y}).

(65)

We assume
\cos(\theta)\neq 0
. Using Lemma
A.8
, we consider the two cases for
(\cos(\theta),\sin(\theta))
in Problem (
65
).

Case a)
.
\cos({\theta})=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+\tan^{2}(\theta)}}
, and
\sin({\theta})=\tfrac{\tan(\theta)}{\sqrt{1+\tan^{2}(\theta)}}
. Problem (
11
) reduces to:

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{+}\in\arg\min_{\theta}\tfrac{a+\tan(\theta)b}{\sqrt{1+\tan^{2}(\theta)}}+\tfrac{w+\tan(\theta)d}{{1+\tan^{2}(\theta)}}+h(\tfrac{\mathbf{x}+\tan(\theta)\mathbf{y}}{\sqrt{1+\tan^{2}(\theta)}}).

Defining
t=\tan(\theta)
, we have the following equivalent problem:

\displaystyle\bar{t}_{+}\in\arg\min_{t}\tfrac{a+bt}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}+\tfrac{w+dt}{{1+t^{2}}}+h(\tfrac{\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}t}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}).

Therefore, the optimal solution
\bar{\theta}_{+}
can be computed as:

\displaystyle\cos(\bar{\theta}_{+})=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+(\bar{t}_{+})^{2}}},~\sin(\bar{\theta}_{+})=\tfrac{\bar{t}_{+}}{\sqrt{1+(\bar{t}_{+})^{2}}}.

(66)

Case b)
.
\cos({\theta})=\tfrac{-1}{\sqrt{1+\tan(\theta)^{2}}}
, and
\sin({\theta})=\tfrac{-\tan(\theta)}{\sqrt{1+\tan(\theta)^{2}}}
. Problem (
11
) boils down to:

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{-}\in\arg\min_{\theta}\tfrac{-a-\tan(\theta)b}{\sqrt{1+\tan(\theta)^{2}}}+\tfrac{w+\tan(\theta)d}{{1+\tan(\theta)^{2}}}+h(\tfrac{-\mathbf{x}-\tan(\theta)\mathbf{y}}{\sqrt{1+\tan(\theta)^{2}}}).

Defining
t=\tan(\theta)
, we have the following equivalent problem:

\displaystyle\bar{t}_{-}\in\arg\min_{t}\tfrac{-a-bt}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}+\tfrac{w+dt}{{1+t^{2}}}+h(\tfrac{-\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}t}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}}}).

Therefore, the optimal solution
\bar{\theta}_{-}
can be computed as:

\displaystyle\cos(\bar{\theta}_{-})=\tfrac{-1}{\sqrt{1+(\bar{t}_{-})^{2}}},~\sin(\bar{\theta}_{-})=\tfrac{-\bar{t}_{-}}{\sqrt{1+(\bar{t}_{-})^{2}}}

(67)

In view of (
66
) and (
67
), when
\cos(\theta)\neq 0
, the optimal solution
\bar{\theta}
for Problem (
65
) is computed as:
[\cos(\bar{\theta}),\sin(\bar{\theta})]\in\arg\min_{c,s}\,\breve{F}(c,s),\,s.t.\,[c,s]\in\{[\cos(\bar{\theta}_{+}),\sin(\bar{\theta}_{+})],[\cos(\bar{\theta}_{-}),\sin(\bar{\theta}_{-})]\}
. Taking into account the case when
\cos(\theta)=0
, the optimal solution
\bar{\theta}
for Problem (
65
) is computed as:

\displaystyle~~~~~~[\cos(\bar{\theta}),\sin(\bar{\theta})]\in\arg\min_{c,s}\,\breve{F}(c,s),

\displaystyle s.t.\,[c,s]\in\{[\cos(\bar{\theta}_{+}),\sin(\bar{\theta}_{+})],[\cos(\bar{\theta}_{-}),\sin(\bar{\theta}_{-})],[0,1],[0,-1]\}.

Notably,
\{\cos(\bar{\theta}),\sin(\bar{\theta})\}
uniquely determines
\bar{\theta}
. Moreover, since the objective function in Problem (
11
) solely depends on
\{\cos(\theta),\sin(\theta)\}
, computing the exact values of
\bar{\theta}_{+}
for (
66
) and
\bar{\theta}_{-}
for (
67
) is unnecessary.

∎

Appendix I
Proof for Appendix Section
D

I.1
Proof of Lemma
D.1

Proof.

(a)
The proof is similar to that of Theorem
3.6
. We omit the proof for brevity.

(b)
Note that the matrix
\mathbf{S}
is an anti-symmetric matrix with
\mathbf{S}=-\mathbf{S}^{\mathsf{T}}
and
\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{S})=\mathbf{0}
. By observing
[\bar{i},\bar{j}]=\arg\max_{i\in[n],j\in[n],i\neq j}|\mathbf{S}_{ij}|
, we can conclude that:

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}(\bar{i},\bar{j})=0~~\Leftrightarrow~~\mathbf{S}=\mathbf{0}.

∎

I.2
Proof of Theorem
D.2

Proof.

We define
\texttt{{B}}=[i,j]
.

We define
c_{1}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj}
,
c_{2}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ij}-\mathbf{T}_{ji}
,
c_{3}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{jj}-\mathbf{T}_{ii}
and
c_{4}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ij}+\mathbf{T}_{ji}
.

(a)
We now focus on the following optimization problem

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{ij}=\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)}\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{2},\mathbf{T}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle.

(68)

We consider two cases for
\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(2,2)
.

Case a)
. When
\mathbf{V}
is a rotation matrix with
\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{rot}}_{\theta}=\begin{bmatrix}\cos(\theta)&\sin(\theta)\\
-\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)\\
\end{bmatrix}
for some suitable
\theta
, we have:

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{2},\mathbf{T}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle

(69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\theta}\,\langle\begin{bmatrix}\cos(\theta)-1&\sin(\theta)\\
-\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)-1\\
\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{T}_{ii}&\mathbf{T}_{ij}\\
\mathbf{T}_{ji}&\mathbf{T}_{jj}\\
\end{bmatrix}\rangle.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\theta}\,\cos(\theta)(\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj})+\sin(\theta)(\mathbf{T}_{ij}-\mathbf{T}_{ji})-(\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{(\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj})^{2}+(\mathbf{T}_{ij}-\mathbf{T}_{ji})^{2}}-(\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{c_{1}^{2}+c_{2}^{2}}-c_{1}.

where step ① uses Lemma
A.9
with
A=\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj}
and
B=\mathbf{T}_{ij}-\mathbf{T}_{ji}
; step ② uses the definition of
c_{1}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj}
and
c_{2}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ij}-\mathbf{T}_{ji}
.

Case b)
. When
\mathbf{V}
is a reflection matrix with
\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{V}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\theta}=\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(\theta)&\sin(\theta)\\
\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)\\
\end{bmatrix}
for some suitable
\theta
, we have:

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},\mathbf{T}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle

(70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\theta}\,\langle\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(\theta)-1&\sin(\theta)\\
\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)-1\\
\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{T}_{ii}&\mathbf{T}_{ij}\\
\mathbf{T}_{ji}&\mathbf{T}_{jj}\\
\end{bmatrix}\rangle.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\theta}\,\cos(\theta)(\mathbf{T}_{jj}-\mathbf{T}_{ii})+\sin(\theta)(\mathbf{T}_{ij}+\mathbf{T}_{ji})-(\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{=}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{(\mathbf{T}_{jj}-\mathbf{T}_{ii})^{2}+(\mathbf{T}_{ij}+\mathbf{T}_{ji})^{2}}-(\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{c_{3}^{2}+c_{4}^{2}}-c_{1}.

where step ① uses Lemma
A.9
with
A=\mathbf{T}_{jj}-\mathbf{T}_{ii}
and
B=\mathbf{T}_{ij}+\mathbf{T}_{ji}
; step ② uses the definition of
c_{3}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{jj}-\mathbf{T}_{ii}
,
c_{4}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ij}+\mathbf{T}_{ji}
, and
c_{1}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ii}+\mathbf{T}_{jj}
.

In view of Equations (
68
), (
69
), and (
70
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{ij}=\min(-\sqrt{c_{1}^{2}+c_{2}^{2}}-c_{1},-\sqrt{c_{3}^{2}+c_{4}^{2}}-c_{1}).

(b)
We note that
h(\mathbf{X})=0
for all
\mathbf{X}
based on our assumption. If
\mathbf{X}^{t}
is not a critical point, then the matrix
\mathbf{G}^{t}[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is not symmetric, and the matrix
\mathbf{T}\triangleq\mathbf{G}^{t}[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}-L\mathbf{X}^{t}[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}-\alpha\mathbf{I}_{n}
is also not symmetric. There exists
\texttt{{B}}=[i,j]
with
i\neq j
such that
\mathbf{T}_{ij}\neq\mathbf{T}_{ji}
, and
c_{2}\triangleq\mathbf{T}_{ij}-\mathbf{T}_{ji}\neq 0
. Consequently,
\mathbf{S}_{ij}=\min(w_{1},w_{2})
becomes
strictly negative
, as
w_{1}=-c_{1}-\sqrt{c_{1}^{2}+c_{2}^{2}}<0
. Since the pair
[\bar{i},\bar{j}]\in\arg\min_{i,j}\mathbf{S}(i,j)
is chosen, we conclude that
\mathbf{S}(\bar{i},\bar{j})<0
.

We now prove that a strict decrease is guaranteed with
F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})<F(\mathbf{X}^{t})
for
OBCD
if
\mathbf{X}^{t}
is not a critical point. We define
\mathcal{X}^{t}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V})\triangleq\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}
. Since
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}
is the global optimal solution of the problem
\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\in\arg\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{V};\mathbf{X}^{t},\texttt{{B}})
, we have:

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}+\langle\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle

(71)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle,~\forall\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k).

We derive the following inequalities:

\displaystyle~F(\mathbf{X}^{t+1})-F(\mathbf{X}^{t})=f(\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\mathbf{V}))-f(\mathbf{X}^{t})

(72)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\langle\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bar{\mathbf{V}}^{t}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}+\alpha\mathbf{I}}^{2}+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle,~\forall\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k).

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\underbrace{\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\mathbf{V})-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}+\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle\right)}_{\triangleq\Xi(\mathbf{V})},

where step ① uses Inequality (
2
); step ② uses Inequality (
71
); step ③ uses the fact that
\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathbf{Q}}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\mathbf{V})-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}
.

We now prove that the right-hand side of (
72
) is consistently negative with the following inequalities:

\displaystyle~\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\Xi(\mathbf{V})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{①}}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\tfrac{L_{f}}{2}\|\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\mathbf{V})-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}-\alpha\mathbf{I}_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\text{②}}{=}

\displaystyle~\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}L_{f}\langle\mathbf{I}_{k}-\mathbf{V},\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}^{t}[\mathbf{X}^{t}]^{\mathsf{T}}\mathrm{U}_{\texttt{{B}}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},[\nabla f(\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}]_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}-\alpha\mathbf{I}_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\text{③}}{=}

\displaystyle~\min_{\mathbf{V}\in\mathrm{St}(k,k)}\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},\mathbf{T}_{\texttt{{B}}\texttt{{B}}}\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{\text{④}}{<}

\displaystyle~0,

where step ① uses
\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}_{\texttt{{B}}}^{t}(\mathbf{V})-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\leq\tfrac{L_{f}}{2}\|\mathcal{X}^{t}_{\texttt{{B}}}(\mathbf{V})-\mathbf{X}^{t}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}
as
\|\mathbf{H}\|_{\mathsf{sp}}\leq L_{f}
, and the identity
\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}=-\langle\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{I}_{k},\alpha\mathbf{I}_{k}\rangle
, which is due to Claim (
c
) of Lemma
2.2
; step ② uses Claim (
b
) of Lemma
2.2
; step ③ uses the definition of
\mathbf{T}\triangleq(\mathbf{G}^{t}-L_{f}\mathbf{X}^{t})(\mathbf{X}^{t})^{\mathsf{T}}-\alpha\mathbf{I}_{n}
in Algorithm
2
; step ④ uses Claim (
a
) of this theorem.

∎

Appendix J
Additional Experiments

In this section, we present the experimental results of the proposed
OBCD
algorithm on the three tasks, namely
\ell_{0}
norm-based SPCA,
\ell_{1}
norm-based SPCA, and Nonnegative PCA using different working set selection strategies.

J.1
Applications to
\ell_{0}
norm-based SPCA, Nonnegative PCA, and
\ell_{1}
norm-based SPCA

Since we have introduced
\ell_{0}
norm-based SPCA in Section
6
, we now present nonnegative PCA and
\ell_{1}
norm-based SPCA.

\blacktriangleright

Nonnegative PCA
Nonnegative PCA is an extension of PCA that imposes nonnegativity constraints on the principal vector
Zass & Shashua 2006
;
Qian et al. 2021
. This constraint leads to a nonnegative representation of loading vectors and it helps to capture data locality in feature selection. Nonnegative PCA can formulated as:

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)}~-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\rangle,\,s.t.\,\mathbf{X}\geq\mathbf{0},

where
\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is the covariance matrix of the data.

\blacktriangleright

L_{1}
Norm-based SPCA
. As the
L_{1}
norm provides the tightest convex relaxation for the
L_{0}
-norm over the unit ball in the sense of
L_{\infty}
-norm, some researchers replace the non-convex and discontinuous
L_{0}
norm function with a convex but non-smooth function
Chen et al. 2016
;
Vu et al. 2013
;
Lu & Zhang 2012
. This leads to the following optimization problem of
L_{1}
norm-based SPCA:

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}\rangle+\lambda\|\mathbf{X}\|_{1},

where
\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is the covariance matrix of the data, and
\lambda>0
.

J.2
Experiment Setting

We compare the objective values
(F(\mathbf{X})-F_{\text{min}})
for different methods after running
t
seconds with
t
varying from
20
to
60
, where the constant
F_{\text{min}}
denotes the smallest objective of all the methods.

Initializations
. We use the same initializations for all methods.
(i)
For the
\ell_{0}
and
\ell_{1}
norm-based SPCA tasks, since the optimal solutions are expected to be sparse, we simply set
\mathbf{X}^{0}\in\mathrm{St}(n,r)
to an identity matrix with
\mathbf{X}^{0}_{ij}=1
if
i=j
and otherwise 0.
(ii)
For the nonnegative PCA task, we use a random nonnegative orthogonal matrix as
\mathbf{X}^{0}
, which can be generated using the following strategy. We first randomly and uniformly partition the index vector
[1,2,...,n]
into
r
nonempty groups
\{\mathcal{G}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r}
with
\mathcal{G}_{i}
being the index vector for the
i
-
th
group, then we set
\mathbf{X}^{0}(\mathcal{G}_{i},i)=\tfrac{1}{|\mathcal{G}_{i}|}
for all
i\in[r]
, where
|\mathcal{G}_{i}|
is the number of elements for the
i
-
th
group.

Variants of OBCD
. We consider three variants of
OBCD
using different working set selection strategies:
(i)

OBCD-R
that uses a simple random strategy;
(ii)

OBCD-CV
that uses a greedy strategy based on maximum stationarity violation pair, and
(iii)

OBCD-OR
that uses a greedy strategy based on objective reduction violation pair. We only consider
|\texttt{{B}}|=k=2
. In order to solve the subproblem for the
\ell_{0}
norm-based SPCA,
\ell_{1}
norm-based SPCA, and nonnegative PCA tasks, we use a breakpoint searching method as presented in Section
5
and Section
B
.

J.3
Experiment Results on Three Tasks

\blacktriangleright

\ell_{0}
Norm-based Sparse PCA

We compare
OBCD
against two state-of-the-art methods:
(i)
Linearized Alternating Direction Method of Multiplier (LADMM)
Lai & Osher 2014
and
(ii)
Smoothing Penalty Method (SPM)
Lai & Osher 2014
;
Chen 2012
. We also initialize
OBCD-R
with the result of LADMM (or SPM) and ran it for 10 seconds to evaluate its effectiveness in improving the solution, leading to
LADMM+OBCD-R
(or
SPM+OBCD-R
). To compute the subgradient
\mathbf{G}^{t}\in\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})
at
\mathbf{X}^{t}
for Algorithm
2
, we choose
\mathbf{G}^{t}=-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathbf{0}
as
\mathbf{0}
is the subgradient of the function
\lambda\|\mathbf{X}\|_{0}
.

Figure
3
shows the convergence curve of the compared methods with
\lambda=100
. Table
2
,
3
, and
4
show the objective values
(F(\mathbf{X})-F_{\text{min}})
for different methods with varying
\lambda\in\{1,300,1000\}
. Several conclusions can be drawn.
(i)
Due to the use of greedy strategy,
OBCD-CV
and
OBCD-OR
often lead to faster convergence than
OBCD-R
for this task.
(ii)

OBCD-R
often greatly improves upon LADMM and SPM; this is because our methods find stronger stationary points than LADMM and SPM.
(iii)
The proposed methods generally deliver the best performance.

\blacktriangleright

Nonnegative PCA

We compare
OBCD
against two state-of-the-art methods:
(i)
Linearized Alternating Direction Method of Multiplier (LADMM)
He & Yuan 2012
;
Lai & Osher 2014
and
(ii)
Smoothing Penalty Method (SPM). We also initialize
OBCD-R
with the result of LADMM (or SPM) and ran it for 10 seconds to evaluate its effectiveness in improving the solution, leading to
LADMM+OBCD-R
(or
SPM+OBCD-R
). To compute the subgradient
\mathbf{G}^{t}\in\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})
at
\mathbf{X}^{t}
for Algorithm
2
, we choose
\mathbf{G}^{t}=-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}^{t}+\mathbf{0}
as
\mathbf{0}
is the subgradient of
\mathcal{I}_{\geq 0}(\mathbf{X}^{t})
.

Table
5
shows the comparisons of objective values and the violation of the constraints
(F(\mathbf{X})-F_{\text{min}},\|\min(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{F}}+\|\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\mathsf{F}})
for different methods. Two conclusions can be drawn.
(i)

OBCD-CV
and
OBCD-OR
are not as effective as
OBCD-R
in this task. This is because the matrix
\mathbf{0}
may not be a suitable choice for the subgradient for the nonsmooth function
\mathcal{I}_{\geq 0}(\mathbf{X})
.
(ii)
Feasibility of our methods is achieved with
\|\min(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{X})\|_{\mathsf{F}}+\|\mathbf{X}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\leq 10^{-12}
. This is because
OBCD
is a feasible method.
(iii)
The proposed methods generally give the best performance.
(iv)

OBCD-R
often greatly improve upon LADMM and SPM, as our methods find stronger stationary points than LADMM and SPM.

\blacktriangleright

\ell_{1}
Norm-based Sparse PCA

We compare
OBCD
against the following state-of-the-art methods: (i) Linearized Alternating Direction Method of Multiplier (LADMM)
He & Yuan 2012
; (ii) ADMM
Lai & Osher 2014
; (iii) Riemannian Subgradient Method (SubGrad)
Li et al. 2021
; (iv) Manifold Proximal Gradient Method (ManPG)
Chen et al. 2020
. We also initialize
OBCD-R
with the result of LADMM (or ManPG) and ran it for 10 seconds to evaluate its effectiveness in improving the solution, leading to
LADMM+OBCD-R
(or
ManPG+OBCD-R
). To compute the subgradient
\mathbf{G}^{t}\in\partial F(\mathbf{X}^{t})
at
\mathbf{X}^{t}
for Algorithm
2
, we choose
\mathbf{G}^{t}=-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}^{t}+\lambda\mathrm{sign}(\mathbf{X}^{t})
as
\mathrm{sign}(\mathbf{X})
is the subgradient of
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{1}
.

Table
6
,
7
, and
8
show the comparisons of objective values
(F(\mathbf{X})-F_{\text{min}})
for different methods with varying
\lambda\in\{1,100,1000\}
. Several conclusions can be drawn.
(i)
ManPG is generally faster than LADMM, ADMM and SubGrad. This is consistent with the reported results in
Chen et al. 2020
.
(ii)

OBCD-OR
outperforms the other methods
\{
LADMM, ADMM, SubGrad, ManPG
\}
by achieving lower objective values.
(iii)

OBCD-OR
often greatly improve upon the LADMM and SPM.

(F(\mathbf{X})-F_{\text{min}})
of

\lambda=1000
. The
1^{st}
,
2^{nd}
, and
3^{rd}
best results are colored with
red
,
green
and
blue
, respectively. If the objective values of ‘LADMM+OBCD-R’ (or ‘ManPG+OBCD-R’) are smaller than those of ‘LADMM’ (or ‘ManPG’) by a margin of
0.1\times a
, where
a
represents the objective values of ‘LADMM’ (or ‘ManPG’), they will be marked with
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0,0}(+)}
.
